So much so that they did it three (3) different ways.</p> <p>Before you ask your instructor for a new lab partner, <b>tell them which table is correct AND whether they can reject or fail to reject the null hypothesis</b> using the information provided.</p>	<span style="color: #5d3b00;"><strong>Table 1</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = 1.067 is correct and we <span style="color: #e60000;"><strong>REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect	<span style="color: #004d99;"><strong>Table 2</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = -0.267 is correct and we <span style="color: #e60000;"><strong>REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect	<span style="color: #b30077;"><strong>Table 3</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = 1.449 is correct and we <span style="color: #e60000;"><strong>REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect	<span style="color: #5d3b00;"><strong>Table 1</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = 1.067 is correct and we <span style="color: #009900;"><strong>FAIL to REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Correct	<span style="color: #004d99;"><strong>Table 2</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = -0.267 is correct and we <span style="color: #009900;"><strong>FAIL to REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect	<span style="color: #b30077;"><strong>Table 3</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = 1.449 is correct and we <span style="color: #009900;"><strong>FAIL to REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect

So much so that they did it three (3) different ways.</p> <p>Before you ask your instructor for a new lab partner, <b>tell them which table is correct AND whether they can reject or fail to reject the null hypothesis</b> using the information provided.</p>	<span style="color: #5d3b00;"><strong>Table 1</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = 19.000 is correct and we <span style="color: #e60000;"><strong>REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Correct	<span style="color: #004d99;"><strong>Table 2</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = 1.257 is correct and we <span style="color: #e60000;"><strong>REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect	<span style="color: #b30077;"><strong>Table 3</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = 0.533 is correct and we <span style="color: #e60000;"><strong>REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect	<span style="color: #5d3b00;"><strong>Table 1</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = 19.000 is correct and we <span style="color: #009900;"><strong>FAIL to REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect	<span style="color: #004d99;"><strong>Table 2</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = 1.257 is correct and we <span style="color: #009900;"><strong>FAIL to REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect	<span style="color: #b30077;"><strong>Table 3</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = 0.533 is correct and we <span style="color: #009900;"><strong>FAIL to REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect
